## [10k GitHub Repos Found Distributing Trojan Malware](https://orchidfiles.com/github-repositories-distributing-malware/) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A security researcher discovered approximately 10,000 GitHub repositories distributing Trojan malware, targeting automated agents rather than human users. The malicious repositories are frequently updated to appear in search results. This widespread supply chain attack poses a significant threat to the open-source ecosystem, as automated agents and developers may unknowingly incorporate compromised dependencies. The scale (10,000 repos) indicates a coordinated campaign that could lead to widespread infections. The malware is designed to evade detection by frequently deleting and pushing new commits, making it appear as recently updated repositories. The attack specifically targets automated agents that crawl GitHub for dependencies, rather than human users.

hackernews · theorchid · Jun 18, 11:45 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48583928)

**Background**: Trojan malware disguises itself as legitimate software to trick users or automated systems into executing it. Supply chain attacks exploit trust in third-party components, as seen in incidents like SolarWinds, where compromised software updates led to widespread breaches.

## [Noam Shazeer Leaves Google for OpenAI](https://twitter.com/NoamShazeer/status/2067400851438932297) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Noam Shazeer, co-author of the seminal 'Attention Is All You Need' paper and former Gemini co-lead at Google, has left Google to join OpenAI, as announced via his X (formerly Twitter) account. This move reshapes the AI talent landscape, as Shazeer is a foundational figure behind the Transformer architecture that powers modern LLMs like GPT and Gemini. His joining OpenAI could accelerate their research and intensify competition with Google. Shazeer originally left Google in 2021 to co-found Character.AI, but returned in 2024 via a licensing/talent deal reportedly worth $2.7 billion, becoming Gemini co-lead. His departure now marks a second exit from Google in just over a year.

**Background**: The Transformer architecture, introduced in the 2017 paper 'Attention Is All You Need', revolutionized deep learning by replacing recurrent layers with self-attention mechanisms, enabling more efficient training of large language models. Shazeer was one of eight co-authors of that paper. Gemini is Google's family of multimodal large language models, announced in December 2023, which competes with OpenAI's GPT series.

## [NAVI-Orbital: First In-Orbit Zero-Shot VLM for Earth Observation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18271) ⭐️ 9.0/10

On April 16, 2026, NAVI-Orbital achieved the first in-orbit demonstration of a zero-shot vision-language model (Gemma 3) performing autonomous multi-modal inference entirely onboard a LEO spacecraft, using natural-language tasking and LangGraph orchestration. This milestone demonstrates that foundation models can run on satellite-class edge computers, enabling semantic compression of Earth observations in orbit and potentially reducing the downlink bandwidth bottleneck. The system achieved 88.16% accuracy on the AID benchmark, and was validated with live in-orbit captures of uncorrected YAM-9 imagery using hardware-accelerated GPU inference without fine-tuning for the flight instrument.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) like Gemma 3 can perform zero-shot classification and image captioning without task-specific training. LangGraph is a framework for building stateful, multi-agent workflows using graph-based state machines. NAVI-Orbital integrates these technologies to enable autonomous Earth observation.

## [NVIDIA Cosmos 3: Unified Omnimodal World Model for Physical AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02800) ⭐️ 9.0/10

NVIDIA introduced Cosmos 3, a family of omnimodal world models that jointly process and generate language, image, video, audio, and action sequences within a unified mixture-of-transformers architecture. Cosmos 3 achieves state-of-the-art results across understanding and generation tasks, unifying vision-language models, video generators, world simulators, and world-action models into a single framework, which could significantly advance embodied AI and robotics. The post-trained Cosmos 3 models were ranked as the best open-source Text-to-Image and Image-to-Video models by Artificial Analysis, and the best policy model by RoboArena. NVIDIA released code, model checkpoints, curated synthetic datasets, and evaluation benchmarks under the Linux Foundation's OpenMDW-1.1 License.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: World models are AI systems that learn an internal representation of the environment to simulate and predict future states, crucial for physical AI applications like robotics and autonomous driving. Omnimodal models extend multimodal AI by integrating even more modalities (e.g., actions) into a single model, enabling more coherent understanding and generation across different data types.

## [Forced Consent Costs Elkjop €1.8M GDPR Fine](https://www.thatprivacyguy.com/blog/elkjop-forced-consent-fine/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A privacy advocate's complaint about Elkjop forcing customers to consent to marketing as a condition for joining its loyalty program led to a €1.8 million fine under GDPR, issued five years later by the Norwegian Data Protection Authority. This case demonstrates that GDPR enforcement can result in substantial penalties for unlawful consent practices, serving as a warning to companies that bundle consent with service access, and empowering individuals to challenge such practices. The fine was based on Elkjop's explicit statement that marketing consent was a condition for membership, which violates GDPR's requirement for freely given consent. The case took five years from complaint to final decision.

hackernews · speckx · Jun 18, 18:31 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48589501)

**Background**: GDPR requires that consent for data processing be freely given, specific, informed, and unambiguous. Forced consent, where a service is conditional on agreeing to marketing, is generally unlawful. The Norwegian Data Protection Authority (Datatilsynet) enforces GDPR in Norway.

**Discussion**: Commenters shared links to the official decision and discussed broader issues, such as companies forcing anti-privacy policies in job interviews. One commenter noted that in the US, exercising privacy rights often puts individuals at a disadvantage.

## [Hospitals and universities repurpose drugs at 90% lower cost](https://www.kcl.ac.uk/news/hospitals-and-universities-repurposing-drugs-at-90-lower-cost) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Hospitals and universities are repurposing existing drugs for new uses at up to 90% lower cost than developing new drugs, challenging traditional pharmaceutical pricing models. This approach could dramatically reduce healthcare costs and provide affordable treatments for diseases that are unprofitable for pharmaceutical companies, such as rare diseases and conditions like macular degeneration. For example, the cancer drug Avastin (bevacizumab) costs about $50 per dose for treating macular degeneration, while the similar drug Lucentis costs around $1,500 per dose. However, regulatory hurdles and lack of manufacturer consent often prevent widespread adoption of repurposed drugs.

hackernews · giuliomagnifico · Jun 18, 10:33 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48583386)

**Background**: Drug repurposing involves investigating existing drugs for new therapeutic purposes, which is faster and cheaper than developing new drugs. Pharmaceutical companies often modify off-patent drugs slightly to obtain new patents, allowing them to maintain high prices. For instance, esketamine (Spravato) is a modified version of ketamine patented to extend exclusivity.

A new vulnerability called MosaicLeaks has been discovered where LLM-based research agents can inadvertently leak sensitive information when prompted with mosaic-style prompts that combine multiple data sources. This vulnerability poses a significant threat to AI safety and privacy, as research agents are increasingly used to handle confidential data, and the leak could expose proprietary or personal information. The attack exploits the ability of LLMs to synthesize information from multiple prompt segments, allowing an adversary to reconstruct hidden data by carefully crafting prompts that reference the same underlying information.

rss · Hugging Face Blog · Jun 18, 18:13

**Background**: LLM-based research agents are AI systems that can autonomously search, summarize, and analyze information from various sources. Mosaic-style prompts refer to prompts that combine multiple pieces of information in a fragmented way, similar to a mosaic. The MosaicLeaks vulnerability arises when such prompts inadvertently cause the agent to reveal data that should remain confidential.

<a id="item-11"></a>
## [CaVe-VLM-CoT: Modular Framework Reduces VLM Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18385) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CaVe-VLM-CoT introduces a modular reflection-based agentic-RAG framework with a five-stage closed-loop pipeline (Extractor, Retriever, Solver, Citation Injector, Verifier) that enforces evidence-grounded reasoning and reduces hallucinations in Vision-Language Models. It also proposes a suite of 23 component-wise metrics anchored by CaVeScore to jointly measure retrieval quality, citation faithfulness, and cross-modal grounding. This framework directly addresses the persistent hallucination problem in VLMs, which undermines trust in AI systems for critical applications like medical imaging and autonomous driving. By enforcing step-level citation grounding and closed-loop verification, it sets a new standard for interpretability and reliability in multimodal AI. CaVe-VLM-CoT achieves 87.1% accuracy and 56.6% CaVeScore on ScienceQA, and 55.2% accuracy and 35.7% CaVeScore on MMMU (30 subjects) without any architectural or prompt modifications. The framework's Verifier detects ungrounded claims and triggers structured feedback to the Extractor for targeted re-retrieval, forming a closed loop.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) like GPT-4V often generate fluent but visually unfaithful outputs, known as hallucinations. Existing chain-of-thought and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) methods only partially address this issue because they do not enforce step-level citation grounding or route verification failures back to retrieval for correction. Agentic RAG extends traditional RAG by incorporating autonomous agents that can iteratively refine retrieval and reasoning.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/html/2606.18385v1">CaVe-VLM-CoT: An Interpretable Vision-Language Model Framework - arXiv</a></li>
<li><a href="https://arxivsignals.io/papers/2606.18385">CaVe-VLM-CoT: An Interpretable Vision-Language Model Framework - ArXivSignals</a></li>
<li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/what-is/retrieval-augmented-generation/">What is RAG ? - Retrieval-Augmented Generation AI Explained - AWS</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

<a id="item-12"></a>
## [CEO-Bench: Testing LLM Agents on Long-Term Startup Management](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18543) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced CEO-Bench, a benchmark that evaluates language model agents on long-horizon, complex decision-making by simulating running a startup for 500 days. The benchmark tests agents on pricing, marketing, budgeting, and other business tasks through a programmable Python interface. CEO-Bench addresses a critical gap in agent evaluation by focusing on sustained, adaptive decision-making over long horizons, which is essential for real-world applications. The results show that even top models like Claude Opus 4.8 and GPT-5.5 struggle to turn a profit, highlighting the need for further research in long-horizon agent capabilities. The strongest agents write sophisticated code to simulate customer cohorts and mine negotiation history, yet most state-of-the-art models fail to consistently turn a profit. Only Claude Opus 4.8 and GPT-5.5 finish above the $1M starting balance, but neither achieves consistent profitability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Language model agents have become proficient at short-horizon tasks like software engineering and customer service, but real-world challenges require navigating long horizons, acquiring information in noisy environments, adapting to change, and orchestrating multiple objectives. CEO-Bench simulates a startup environment to test these combined capabilities, using a Python interface to manage pricing, marketing, budgeting, and other business aspects.

Researchers introduced DeFAb, a benchmark for defeasible abduction that generates over 372,000 instances from formal knowledge bases, and found that the best frontier language model achieves only 65% accuracy compared to 100% for a rule-based solver. This benchmark reveals a significant gap between neural and symbolic reasoning, highlighting that current foundation models struggle with disciplined theory revision, which is crucial for scientific discovery and creative problem-solving. The benchmark includes three difficulty levels with polynomial-time verifiable gold standards, and a rendering-robust evaluation shows accuracy drops to 7.8-23.5% for frontier models; chain-of-thought variance (~36 percentage points) exceeds inter-model gaps.

This paper formally characterizes what generalist agents must remember to act near-optimally across multiple environments, proving that memory is essential for domain disambiguation and transition-model reconstruction. This work provides a foundational theoretical framework for understanding memory in multi-task reinforcement learning, potentially guiding the design of more efficient and generalizable AI agents. The paper presents a separation theorem showing that uniformly near-optimal policies must induce distinct memory distributions at observational bottlenecks when domains require incompatible actions. It also shows that memory can be used to approximately reconstruct local transition dynamics.

## [BeliefDiffusion: Generative-Model Predictive Planning for Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18888) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BeliefDiffusion introduces a novel framework that combines diffusion models with model predictive control (MPC) to explicitly characterize multimodal belief distributions for navigation in partially observable environments. This work addresses a key challenge in robotics—handling multimodal belief spaces due to perceptual aliasing—and demonstrates significant improvements over existing methods, potentially enabling more robust autonomous navigation in real-world scenarios. The framework operates in two steps: first, it imagines plausible environment configurations based on observation history using a diffusion model, then plans efficient navigation strategies across aggregated configurations via MPC. Experiments on synthetic maps show it outperforms model-free RL and other generative approaches in success rate and path efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Navigation in partially observable environments requires agents to make decisions with limited sensory information, often leading to multimodal belief distributions (multiple possible states). Traditional belief-based methods using neural networks struggle to capture this multimodality, while generative models like diffusion models can represent complex distributions but lack planning mechanisms. Model predictive control (MPC) is a control strategy that optimizes actions over a future horizon subject to constraints.

## [Decoupled Search Grounding: Vendor-Agnostic Architecture for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18947) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Decoupled Search Grounding (DSG), a vendor-agnostic architecture that separates search from reasoning in LLM agents via an MCP-compatible gateway, enabling independent control over provider routing, caching, and fallback. DSG addresses Search-Induced Verbosity and reduces search costs by over 90% while maintaining accuracy, making production LLM agents more controllable, cost-effective, and portable across models and providers. On SimpleQA, DSG achieved 86.1% accuracy vs. native 87.7% but at 91% lower search cost, and reached a 99.4% warm-cache hit rate with 68% lower latency. In an e-commerce QIU workload, it cut search cost by over 98% while matching or exceeding native accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Production LLM agents often rely on real-time search for grounding, but native search bundles retrieval policy, provider choice, and evidence injection into a single model-provider boundary, making it hard to inspect or tune. This coupling can cause Search-Induced Verbosity, where models produce overly verbose outputs that violate strict contracts. MCP (Model Context Protocol) is an open standard for connecting LLMs with external tools and context, analogous to a USB-C port for AI applications.

## [User as Engram: Efficient Per-User Memory via Local Edits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19172) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose 'User as Engram', a method that stores per-user memory as local parametric edits in an Engram model's hash-keyed memory table, achieving a 33,000x smaller memory footprint compared to per-user LoRA adapters. This approach dramatically reduces memory requirements for personalizing large language models while improving indirect reasoning accuracy by 5.6x on average, enabling scalable and efficient AI personalization without contaminating unrelated text. The method separates content (stored as sparse Engram rows) from reasoning skill (carried in a shared adapter), allowing multiple users' edits to compose additively and losslessly in a single shared table, unlike per-user LoRA which uses a single global weight delta.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Engram is a neural memory architecture that uses a hash-keyed table to store sparse local edits, inspired by the brain's hippocampal engram system. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a popular fine-tuning method that trains low-rank weight matrices to adapt LLMs, but it mixes content and reasoning into a single global delta, causing interference. The 'User as Engram' approach leverages Engram's sparsity to store per-user facts without affecting other model behavior.

## [NeSyCat Torch: Differentiable Categorical Semantics for Neuro-Symbolic Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19279) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NeSyCat Torch provides a differentiable tensor implementation of categorical semantics for neurosymbolic learning, using monads to unify classical, fuzzy, probabilistic, and neural systems under a single framework. It introduces the lazy log-tensor monad over the log-semiring for numerically stable, differentiable training, and a batch monad for efficient batch processing. This work bridges a key gap in neurosymbolic AI by enabling neural network-learned predicates and functions within a unified categorical semantics, outperforming existing frameworks like LTN and DeepProbLog on MNIST addition. It offers a principled, modular approach that can be extended to continuous probability and other paradigms, potentially accelerating research in interpretable and hybrid AI systems. The implementation uses the distribution monad for reference semantics and metric evaluation, and the lazy log-tensor monad for differentiable training, with a batch monad for efficiency. On MNIST addition, HaskTorch, JAX, and PyTorch implementations achieve nearly the accuracy of DeepStochLog while staying within a uniform framework applicable to many first-order neurosymbolic approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Neurosymbolic learning combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning, but different semantics (classical, fuzzy, probabilistic) have been defined with separate inductive rules. NeSyCat, extending the ULLER framework, unifies these under a categorical semantics based on monads—a concept from functional programming that models side effects. This work provides the missing neural component by implementing predicates and functions learned by neural networks within the NeSyCat framework.

## [Rubric-Conditioned Self-Distillation for Reasoning LMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19327) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Rubric-Conditioned Self-Distillation (RCSD), a framework that uses structured rubrics to provide fine-grained, token-level feedback for self-distillation in reasoning language models, outperforming GRPO by 1.0 points and OPSD by 0.9 points on science reasoning benchmarks. This method addresses key limitations of existing post-training approaches: it avoids expensive chain-of-thought annotations and provides more granular feedback than scalar reward signals, potentially improving reasoning quality in LLMs more efficiently. The framework uses a two-stage pipeline: first learning to generate task-specific rubrics, then training a rubric-guided reasoner. It conditions the teacher model on criterion-level rubrics to provide token-level guidance on the student's own sampled trajectories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training of reasoning LMs typically relies on supervised distillation (using chain-of-thought annotations) or reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards. Distillation suffers from noisy or incomplete annotations, while RL compresses feedback into a scalar signal, losing fine-grained information. Self-distillation uses the model itself as a teacher, but prior methods lacked structured guidance. RCSD introduces rubrics—structured criteria—to provide dense, per-token supervision.

A new paper identifies 'persona manifold collapse', where richer persona descriptions in LLMs reduce behavioral diversity instead of improving it, and shows that simple Age-Gender personas outperform complex profiles in downstream prediction. This finding challenges common assumptions in AI alignment and social science research that richer personas improve simulation fidelity, highlighting the need for representation-aware persona construction. The effect persists across multiple architectures and scales, and cannot be fully explained by attention saturation or paraphrasing sensitivity; certain attribute combinations form 'alignment bridges' that preserve behavioral stability.

Researchers propose Continuous Audio Thinking (CoAT), a framework that introduces a continuous latent workspace for large audio language models (LALMs) to preserve acoustic information via expert distillation before generating text responses. CoAT addresses a key limitation in LALMs where acoustic details like prosody and sound events are lost during text generation, potentially improving performance across audio reasoning, speech emotion recognition, and music classification tasks. The continuous thinking block can be processed in a single prefill, so CoAT adds no extra autoregressive decoding cost. ## [PROPEL: Training Task Generators Without Expensive Solver Rollouts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18284) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PROPEL introduces a solver-amortized framework that trains task generators at the learnable frontier by using a lightweight activation probe to predict solver pass rates, eliminating the need for repeated solver rollouts during generator optimization. This addresses a critical bottleneck in reinforcement learning training—expensive solver-in-the-loop task generation—enabling scalable generation of frontier tasks for increasingly capable models, particularly in software engineering where solver rollouts can take tens of minutes. PROPEL trains an activation probe on a one-time labeled corpus of generated tasks and solver outcomes, then uses it as a proxy for solve rate during generator optimization, reducing evaluation to a single forward pass. Experiments show improvements in the share of tasks at the targeted solve rate across math, code, and SWE benchmarks.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) trains agents by interacting with an environment, but as models improve, fixed task distributions become saturated. Generating new tasks that are both valid and appropriately difficult (the 'learnable frontier') is essential but costly when each candidate requires a full solver rollout. PROPEL amortizes this cost by learning a proxy that predicts solver performance without running the solver.

DRIFT introduces a method that uses on-policy influence functions to refine the training data distribution for supervised fine-tuning of large language models, aiming to raise the performance ceiling rather than just preserve performance under budget constraints. This work addresses a critical limitation of existing data curation methods, which focus on subset selection under budget constraints, by instead optimizing the data distribution to maximize model capability. It could significantly improve the effectiveness of fine-tuning for LLMs, benefiting a wide range of applications. DRIFT uses the model's own on-policy rollouts as validation targets to overcome the proximity gap and gradient norm bias of standard influence functions. It applies signed weighting based on trajectory correctness and debiases influence scores, enabling a small set of validation queries to reliably attribute the full dataset.

## [TRIDENT: Provably Safe Multi-Agent RL Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18308) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TRIDENT is the first multi-agent reinforcement learning framework that co-designs three components to break the coupling between hybrid actions, safety constraints, and physics dynamics, achieving provable convergence and safety guarantees. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in safety-critical cyber-physical systems, enabling reliable multi-agent coordination with theoretical guarantees, which could impact autonomous driving, drone swarms, and smart infrastructure. TRIDENT includes a Richardson-Romberg gradient correction that reduces Gumbel-Softmax bias from O(τ) to O(τ²), a Lyapunov-constrained sequential trust-region update for per-iterate feasibility, and a physics-informed residual critic. It achieves an O~(1/√K) convergence rate and O(√K) cumulative-violation bound.

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) for cyber-physical systems must handle hybrid discrete-continuous actions, safety constraints during training, and physics-governed dynamics. These three features form a directed cycle of biases that naive composition of off-the-shelf modules cannot resolve. TRIDENT's co-designed components cancel each leak, providing provable safety and convergence.

## [Agentra: Supervisable Multi-Agent Framework for Intrusion Response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18325) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Agentra is a novel multi-agent framework that converts security alerts from IDS, EDR, and XDR platforms into structured incident response plans grounded in MITRE ATT&CK, MITRE D3FEND, and NIST CSF 2.0, improving the F1 score from 0.61 to 0.84 over static playbooks. This framework addresses a critical gap in automated incident response by enabling human supervision and auditability, which are essential for enterprise security operations. It significantly outperforms static playbooks, reducing response delays and improving coverage. Agentra uses a bounded Planner-Validator review loop, a Moderator security gateway for threat intelligence, an Action Catalog with risk scoring, and an append-only audit log. Evaluated on a 120-event corpus from ThreatHunter-Playbook, Splunk BOTSv3, and DARPA OpTC, the best configuration achieved an F1 of 0.84 and a harmful-action rate of 0.0%.

## [SafeClawBench: Staged Benchmark for LLM Agent Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18356) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SafeClawBench is a new staged benchmark with 600 adversarial tasks that evaluates security failures in tool-using LLM agents across three separate endpoints: semantic attack acceptance, audit-visible harm evidence, and sandbox-observed tool/state harm. Existing evaluations often collapse different failure stages into a single attack success rate, making it hard to distinguish textual compliance from actual harm; SafeClawBench provides nuanced insights that are critical for improving AI agent safety and security. The benchmark covers six attack families including direct/indirect prompt injection, tool-return injection, memory poisoning, memory extraction, and ambiguity-driven unsafe inference. In a 12,000-row matched analysis, 291 of 347 observed sandbox harms occurred in rows that passed the semantic check.

This paper introduces a variational framework for regulated language generation, modeling the interaction between a generator and a regulator as a saddle-point problem with an entropy-regularized Gibbs law and f-divergence convex dual. This framework provides a theoretical foundation for important AI safety applications such as content moderation, censorship, and deception detection, clarifying the tradeoffs between utility, entropy, regulatory alignment, and detectability. The effectiveness of these methods has been debated, and Veriphi provides systematic evidence that it depends on the dataset.

## [Logit Scale, Not Weight Norm, Controls Grokking Delay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18465) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that the grokking delay in neural networks is primarily mediated by the logit scale under cross-entropy loss, not the weight norm itself. By clamping weight norms and varying output temperature, the authors show that the logit scale alone explains 97% of the variance in grokking delay. This finding refines the understanding of grokking—a phenomenon where networks suddenly generalize after prolonged memorization—by identifying the proximal variable (logit scale) rather than the commonly assumed weight norm. It has implications for designing training algorithms and interpreting generalization dynamics in deep learning. The study uses modular addition tasks and controls for rescaling artifacts, showing that the logit scale mediates the effect of weight norm on grokking delay under cross-entropy loss. Under mean-squared error loss, the logit scale is pinned and the norm acts through a different route.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Grokking refers to the phenomenon where a neural network suddenly transitions from memorizing training data to generalizing after extended training, often long after overfitting. Previous work has linked the timing of this transition to the weight norm, with smaller norms leading to earlier generalization. This paper challenges that view by showing that the weight norm is merely an upstream handle, while the logit scale—the magnitude of outputs before softmax—is the direct controller.

## [Code-Augur: Agentic Vulnerability Detection via Specification Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18619) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Code-Augur introduces a security-specification-first paradigm that exposes an LLM agent's tacit assumptions as explicit security specifications and continuously refines them via runtime falsification using a guided fuzzer. This approach addresses the critical opacity of agent reasoning in vulnerability detection, improving trust and effectiveness. It discovered 22 new vulnerabilities in key open-source projects, outperforming state-of-the-art agents and specialized models. Code-Augur works by analyzing each code component and, when deemed secure, committing local invariants as in-source assertions. A guided fuzzer then attempts to falsify these assertions, revealing either genuine vulnerabilities or flawed specifications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic vulnerability detection uses autonomous LLM agents to audit code for security flaws. However, the reasoning behind agent judgments is often opaque and unvalidated, leading to missed vulnerabilities. Specification inference aims to make assumptions explicit, while runtime falsification tests those assumptions against actual program behavior.

## [CURE: Context Management for Tabular Foundation Models in Stream Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18677) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose CURE, a context management policy for tabular foundation models in stream learning, achieving up to 27% relative improvement over classical methods across seven data streams. This work shifts the challenge from model updating to context management, offering a principled framework that significantly improves stream learning performance and is robust across different tabular foundation model backbones. CURE uses entropy-gated admission and redundancy-aware eviction to manage context, satisfying three requirements: preserve recent examples, retain uncertain examples, and remove redundant examples. Code and datasets are available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Tabular foundation models (TFMs) make predictions conditioned on a labeled context in an in-context manner, making them natural for stream learning where data arrives sequentially under distribution shift. Traditional stream learning methods adapt by updating model states, but TFMs shift the challenge to context management. CURE addresses this by introducing a future information view that yields practical requirements for context management.

## [DCGWM Prevents Objective Interference Collapse in JEPA World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18688) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Dual-Channel Grounded World Modeling (DCGWM), a novel architecture that structurally prevents Objective Interference Collapse (OIC) in JEPA-based world models by partitioning the latent space into physical and behavioral subspaces with inward-only gradient flow. OIC is a newly identified failure mode where joint learning of heterogeneous signals (physical and social-behavioral) in a shared latent space causes one channel to collapse the other's representation, which cannot be fixed by loss weighting alone. DCGWM offers a principled solution that could improve robustness and safety in AI world models for robotics, autonomous driving, and multi-agent systems. DCGWM uses a partitioned latent space with a physical subspace Z_p updated via VICReg-style alignment and a behavioral subspace Z_b updated via multi-agent simulation trajectories, coupled by an Inter-Channel Interface Module without cross-subspace gradients. An Asymmetric Grounding Adherence Loss uses a hard hinge for physical violations and a soft KL divergence for behavioral divergence, and the Generative Rendering Layer is architecturally isolated from the latent world model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Joint Embedding Predictive Architectures (JEPAs) learn representations by predicting embeddings of future observations in a latent space, rather than predicting pixels directly. VICReg is a self-supervised learning method that regularizes embeddings to have high variance, invariance to augmentations, and decorrelated dimensions. Objective Interference Collapse (OIC) is a newly identified failure mode where jointly learning from two qualitatively different signals (e.g., physical dynamics and social-behavioral dynamics) in a shared latent space causes the dominant signal to collapse the representation of the subordinate one.

## [Morpheus: Neural Tokenizer for Turkish Morphology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18717) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Morpheus is a neural tokenizer and word embedder for Turkish that uses a differentiable Poisson-binomial dynamic program to achieve lossless, morphology-aware segmentation, producing structured embeddings in a single forward pass. This addresses a key limitation of current subword tokenizers (e.g., BPE, WordPiece) that fragment morphemes in agglutinative languages like Turkish, enabling better morphological alignment and reversible tokenization crucial for generative tasks. Morpheus achieves the lowest bits-per-character (1.425) among reversible tokenizers, roughly doubles morphological alignment (MorphScore macro-F1 0.61 vs. ~0.32), and uses ~19% less GPU memory than 64K-vocabulary subword tokenizers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Turkish is an agglutinative language where words are formed by concatenating morphemes (meaningful units). Standard subword tokenizers like BPE or WordPiece split words based on frequency, often breaking morphemes and losing information, and are not reversible (decode(encode(w)) ≠ w). Morpheus uses a differentiable dynamic program over per-character boundary probabilities to learn morpheme boundaries end-to-end, ensuring lossless encoding.

Researchers introduced RedactionBench, a manually annotated benchmark of 200 documents across 11 domains for evaluating PII redaction in LLMs, along with a novel character-level metric R-Score that treats semantically similar redactions equally. This benchmark addresses the gap between simple entity recognition and context-dependent privacy redaction, grounded in contextual integrity theory, and reveals that current models struggle with contextual redaction, motivating future privacy-preserving systems. RedactionBench operationalizes this theory for LLM evaluation.

SC-GRPO uses the model's own verified trajectories to assign per-token credit without external teachers or reward models, outperforming GRPO by 8.1% and DAPO by 5.9% on five benchmarks. This method addresses a key limitation in token-level credit assignment for LLM reasoning, enabling more efficient training and better performance without relying on external resources. SC-GRPO computes per-token KL divergence between the original and self-conditioned distributions, using it as a multiplicative weight on GRPO gradients. The paper provides theoretical proof that distilling from multiple verified trajectories leads to infeasible weighted-average solutions.

## [RL Foundation Models: A Call for Synthetic MDP Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18812) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new position paper argues that reinforcement learning lacks foundation models due to the absence of synthetic MDP priors, and proposes that MDPs' fixed-size sufficient statistics make them amenable to attention-based architectures. As a proof of concept, the authors train a model entirely on synthetic MDPs that solves held-out tabular benchmarks in context without task-specific tuning. This work identifies a significant gap in the foundation model landscape and proposes a concrete direction for building RL foundation models, potentially enabling in-context learning for reinforcement learning similar to what TabPFN achieved for tabular data. If successful, it could dramatically reduce the need for task-specific RL training and accelerate RL applications across robotics, game playing, and control. The paper argues that sampling a synthetic MDP is as feasible as sampling a synthetic tabular dataset, yet no in-context RL work treats prior design as a primary objective. The proof-of-concept model, trained entirely on synthetic MDPs, outperforms UCB-VI and tabular Q-learning online and competes with VI-LCB offline on held-out benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation models like GPT-4 and TabPFN are pretrained on large-scale data and can be adapted to many tasks without retraining. TabPFN uses a transformer pretrained on synthetic Bayesian priors to solve tabular classification. In-context reinforcement learning (ICRL) aims to adapt RL agents to new tasks via prompt conditioning, but has not leveraged synthetic MDP priors. This paper proposes that MDPs have fixed-size sufficient statistics (tabular in shape) that make them suitable for attention-based architectures, similar to tabular data.

## [Neural Semigroup Superposition Generates Riemannian Metrics for Zero-Shot Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18828) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a neural semigroup superposition architecture that generates a Riemannian metric field directly from a scene, enabling robots to follow geodesics for collision-free motion without a separate planner or collision checker. The model, trained on a single two-obstacle scene, demonstrates robust zero-shot generalization to unseen obstacle configurations. This approach embeds intelligence into the environment's geometry rather than the agent, potentially simplifying robotic motion planning and enabling faster, more scalable navigation. It could impact robotics, autonomous systems, and any domain requiring real-time path planning in complex spaces. The architecture uses an Encoder-Router network with three parameter groups: frame parameters, modulation parameters, and basic coefficients, combined via a semigroup-superposition mechanism. The resulting metric field yields orders-of-magnitude cost separation between collision-free and obstacle-penetrating paths.

**Background**: Riemannian geometry provides a mathematical framework where a manifold is equipped with a metric that defines distances and geodesics (shortest paths). In robotics, motion planning often involves searching over configuration spaces; this work instead learns a metric that makes geodesics naturally avoid obstacles. The semigroup superposition mechanism allows combining multiple local metric contributions into a global field.

## [TRAP: Benchmark for AI Agent Privacy vs. Task Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18996) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced TRAP (Task-completion and Resistance to Active Privacy-extraction), a benchmark that evaluates the trade-off between task accuracy and resistance to active privacy extraction in AI agents handling sensitive documents. They tested 22 models and found all exhibit non-trivial leakage, with instruction-following ability correlating with leakage rate. This benchmark addresses a critical tension in deploying AI agents that must use private data for tasks while preventing leakage, which is increasingly relevant as agents handle sensitive information like passport numbers. The impossibility result shows that prompt-based defenses cannot jointly achieve high task success and zero leakage, motivating structural solutions. The benchmark includes scenarios with a document containing private information, a task query requiring tool invocation using private fields, and an attack query attempting to elicit the same information. The paper proves that for any softmax-based model, no soft-constraint defense can jointly achieve high task success with zero leakage probability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents are increasingly used in document-intensive workflows where sensitive private information is routine input, such as booking flights with passport numbers. These agents must use private data to complete tasks accurately but must never expose it in responses, creating a fundamental tension between capability and privacy. Existing prompt-based defenses attempt to mitigate leakage but often degrade task performance.

Spotlight introduces a system that uses spot GPUs for reinforcement learning post-training of Diffusion Transformers, tolerating stale model weights during seed exploration to reduce costs by 1.4-6.4x while achieving 4x faster convergence. This work addresses a critical cost bottleneck in DiT RL post-training, which is prohibitively expensive with dedicated GPUs. By enabling cost-effective use of spot instances, it could democratize large-scale AI training for image generation and other applications. Spotlight introduces three techniques: a bandit-based exploration planner, elastic sequence parallelism for on-the-fly SP group reconfiguration, and a preemption-aware pull-based request scheduler. System modeling projects communication and memory benefits to 100B-scale configurations.

OrthoReg works by adding a penalty term that encourages the neural network's output to be orthogonal to the symbolic component's output, ensuring a complementary decomposition. It is particularly effective when the symbolic structure is discovered from data via sparse regression.

The paper introduces Essential Subspace Merging (ESM), a training-free static merging method that decomposes task updates based on principal components of activation shifts to reduce inter-task interference. The method relies on Essential Subspace Decomposition (ESD), which identifies a low-dimensional subspace where task-relevant activation shifts are concentrated. ESM orthogonalizes and fuses essential components, while ESM++ further decomposes residuals into low-rank experts with prototype-based routing.

Researchers propose MAST (Mechanism-Aligned Selective Targeting), a method that selectively unlearns RLVR-induced reasoning in language models by updating only a subset of attention-projection tensors, achieving significant target forgetting with minimal collateral damage. This work addresses a critical challenge in AI safety: removing undesirable reasoning patterns (e.g., from RLVR training) without degrading overall model performance, which is essential for model editing and responsible deployment of large language models. MAST ranks attention-projection tensors by off-principal energy, update magnitude, and forget-gradient coupling magnitude, then updates only the top-ranked subset. On Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B, it reduced MATH forget accuracy from 45/150 to 37/150 (McNemar p=0.0078) while preserving GSM8K and MATH retain performance.

However, once learned, such reasoning patterns can be difficult to remove without retraining. Machine unlearning aims to selectively erase specific knowledge or behaviors from a trained model, but full-parameter gradient ascent often damages retained capabilities. MAST leverages mechanistic insights to identify and update only the parameters most responsible for the target behavior.

## [Recursive Joint Simulation Boosts AI Cooperation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08128) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces recursive joint simulation, where AI agents run nested simulations of a game before acting, and proves this is strategically equivalent to an infinitely repeated game, enabling cooperation via folk theorems. This framework offers a principled way to foster cooperation among AI agents in one-shot interactions, which is crucial for multi-agent AI safety and alignment, as it leverages a key difference between AI and human interactions: the possibility of accurate simulation. The recursion includes a small chance of failure to avoid infinite loops, and the equivalence holds even when assumptions are relaxed and from the perspective of an agent with self-locating uncertainty inside the simulation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: In game theory, the Prisoner's Dilemma shows that rational players often defect, leading to suboptimal outcomes. Repeated play can enable cooperation through strategies like tit-for-tat. This paper extends that idea to single-shot games by using recursive simulation, which creates a repeated-game structure artificially.

## [RippleBench: Automated Benchmark for Ripple Effects in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04144) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced RippleBench-Maker, an automatic pipeline that generates multiple-choice questions from knowledge repositories to measure ripple effects of model editing and unlearning in language models. They instantiated it using WikiRAG to create RippleBench-WMDP-Bio with 584 seed topics and 352,961 questions, and evaluated eight unlearning methods on Llama3-8B-Instruct. This benchmark provides a systematic way to quantify unintended side-effects of model interventions, which is crucial for AI safety and reliability. The finding that ripple effects are a property of the unlearning method rather than the base model has important implications for developing safer editing techniques. The pipeline retrieves semantic neighbors from a knowledge repository and generates questions at varying semantic distances. Experiments across Llama3-8B, Mistral-7B, Zephyr-7B, and Yi-34B showed nearly identical cross-model delta curves, indicating ripple effects depend on the method, not the model.

## [InfoPO: Information-Driven Policy Optimization for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00656) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose InfoPO, a novel reinforcement learning method that uses information-gain rewards to improve credit assignment in multi-turn LLM agent interactions, outperforming existing prompting and multi-turn RL baselines across diverse tasks. This addresses a critical limitation in current multi-turn RL methods for LLM agents—trajectory-level credit assignment—by providing dense, fine-grained learning signals, enabling more efficient and targeted agent learning for real-world underspecified user requests. InfoPO computes an information-gain reward by comparing the agent's action distribution with and without the actual feedback, then fuses it with task outcome rewards via adaptive variance-gated fusion. The method is validated on intent clarification, collaborative coding, and tool-augmented decision making tasks, and shows robustness under user simulator shifts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often need to interact with users over multiple turns to clarify underspecified requests. Current multi-turn reinforcement learning methods, such as GRPO, typically assign rewards only at the trajectory level, making it hard to credit individual turns that contribute to success. InfoPO leverages information theory to provide turn-level credit, similar to how curiosity-driven exploration works in RL, but tailored for agent-user collaboration.

A new paper provides a practical decision framework for selecting and applying seven information-theoretic measures in AI, including entropy, cross-entropy, mutual information, transfer entropy, integrated information (Phi), effective information (EI), and autonomy. This framework fills a gap in measure selection guidance, helping practitioners avoid common misuses and choose appropriate estimators based on data type and dimensionality, which is critical for reliable AI research and applications. The framework is organized around three prescriptive questions per measure: what question it answers, which estimator is appropriate, and the most dangerous misuse. It includes a measure-selection flowchart and a master decision table, with three worked examples.

## [VidCRAFT3: Unified Control of Camera, Object, and Lighting in I2V](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07531) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VidCRAFT3 is a novel image-to-video generation framework that jointly controls camera motion, object motion, and lighting direction by modeling their physical interactions, achieving state-of-the-art performance in control precision and visual coherence. This work addresses a critical limitation in existing controllable video generation methods, which treat camera, object, and lighting independently, leading to visual inconsistencies. By enabling simultaneous and physically coherent control, VidCRAFT3 significantly enhances the realism and flexibility of video content creation for applications like film production and virtual reality. VidCRAFT3 introduces Image2Cloud for explicit 3D geometric priors, ObjMotionNet for encoding sparse object trajectories, and a Spatial Triple-Attention Transformer for lighting cross-attention. It also constructs the VideoLightingDirection (VLD) dataset with per-frame lighting annotations and uses a three-stage progressive training strategy to overcome the scarcity of jointly annotated data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Controllable image-to-video (I2V) generation aims to create a video from a single image guided by user-specified controls like camera motion, object motion, and lighting. Existing methods often treat these controls independently, ignoring their physical coupling—for example, changing the camera viewpoint also affects how shadows and lighting appear on objects. VidCRAFT3 explicitly models these interactions to produce more consistent and realistic videos.

Researchers propose a new zeroth-order optimization method for federated finetuning of large language models that splits the model into blocks and allocates more perturbations to the second block, enabling efficient reuse of intermediate activations to reduce computation by up to 3x compared to prior ZO methods. This work addresses the critical challenge of finetuning large language models on resource-constrained edge devices in federated learning, where memory and computation are limited. By significantly reducing computational demand while preserving convergence benefits, it enables privacy-preserving LLM adaptation on devices like smartphones and IoT hardware. The method splits the model into consecutive blocks and uses a higher number of perturbations for the second block, allowing forward activations from the first block to be reused. Evaluations on RoBERTa-large, OPT1.3B, and LLaMa-3-3.2B models show up to 3x computation reduction over other ZO techniques, while retaining memory and communication benefits of first-order methods.

## [Overtraining Experts Harms Model Merging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14126) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that overtraining expert models during fine-tuning degrades model merging performance, contradicting the assumption that better fine-tuning always benefits merging. This finding challenges a key assumption in the model merging pipeline, which is widely used to combine capabilities from fine-tuned models, and suggests that early stopping can significantly improve merging outcomes. The degradation is traced to memorization of a small set of difficult examples in late fine-tuning, causing negative parameter interference that is forgotten during merging. Researchers introduced SODA (Stereotyped Object Diagnostic Audit), a framework to systematically measure demographic bias in objects generated by text-to-image models, finding that neutral prompts default to middle-aged and White-associated outputs and that demographic cues produce highly skewed stereotypes. This work addresses an underexplored area of bias in generative AI—objects rather than humans—revealing implicit demographic associations that could perpetuate stereotypes in real-world applications like advertising or design. The study analyzed 8,000 images across five state-of-the-art models and eight object categories, using three metrics: Base vs. Demographic Divergence (BDS), Cross-Demographic Disparity (CDS), and Visual Attribute Concentration (VAC). Notably, 26.6% of object-model-demographic combinations produced outputs where all 20 images shared the exact same attribute value.

SAUP generalizes this by producing different outputs based on input semantics.

## [HeRo-Q: Hessian-Based Framework for Stable LLM Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21626) ⭐️ 8.0/10

HeRo-Q introduces a learnable rotation-compression matrix that reshapes the loss landscape by reducing the largest Hessian eigenvalue, enabling stable low-bit quantization for large language models. It achieves superior performance on Llama and Qwen models under W4A8 and W3A16 settings, boosting GSM8K accuracy on Llama3 8B to 70.15%. This work addresses the fundamental 'low error, high loss' paradox in post-training quantization by directly conditioning the Hessian of the loss landscape, which is critical for deploying large language models efficiently on resource-constrained devices. It outperforms existing methods like GPTQ, AWQ, and SpinQuant without requiring architectural changes, making it a practical advancement for model compression. HeRo-Q integrates seamlessly into existing PTQ pipelines with negligible computational overhead, requiring no architectural modifications. It excels in the challenging W3A16 ultra-low-bit regime, effectively avoiding the logical collapse common in aggressive quantization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training quantization (PTQ) compresses neural networks by reducing the precision of weights and activations, but often suffers from the 'low error, high loss' phenomenon where minimizing quantization error does not guarantee low task loss. The Hessian matrix, representing second-order derivatives of the loss function, reveals that a few high-curvature directions are extremely sensitive to perturbations, causing instability. HeRo-Q addresses this by applying a learnable rotation-compression matrix to reshape the loss landscape before quantization.

This paper demonstrates that the loss of plasticity in neural networks during continual learning is significantly less severe when task transitions are gradual rather than abrupt, using input/output interpolation and task sampling. This finding challenges the common practice of using abrupt task transitions in continual learning benchmarks, suggesting that plasticity loss may be an artifact of abruptness rather than an inherent limitation of neural networks. The authors provide both theoretical and empirical analysis, showing that the severity of plasticity loss is closely tied to transition abruptness, and can be substantially reduced in gradually changing environments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning aims to train neural networks on a sequence of tasks without forgetting previous knowledge. Loss of plasticity refers to the network's decreasing ability to learn new tasks over time, which has been observed in many continual learning setups. Most existing benchmarks use abrupt task switches, which may exaggerate the problem.

## [ActMem: Actionable Memory with Causal Reasoning for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00026) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose ActMem, a novel memory framework that transforms unstructured dialogue history into a structured causal and semantic graph, enabling LLM agents to perform counterfactual reasoning and commonsense completion for better decision-making in long-term interactions. Current memory systems treat agents as passive recorders, failing in tasks requiring reasoning; ActMem bridges this gap by integrating active causal reasoning, significantly improving consistency and reliability in complex, memory-dependent tasks. The framework introduces a new benchmark dataset, ActMemEval, specifically designed to evaluate reasoning capabilities beyond fact retrieval. Experiments show ActMem outperforms baselines in handling complex, memory-dependent scenarios.

**Background**: LLM agents often rely on memory systems to store and retrieve past interactions. However, existing memory frameworks typically retrieve information without understanding deeper implications, leading to failures in tasks that require causal reasoning or conflict resolution. ActMem addresses this by building a causal and semantic graph from dialogue history, enabling agents to reason about implicit constraints and counterfactual scenarios.

## [IPSL-AID: Diffusion Models for Climate Downscaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03275) ⭐️ 8.0/10

IPSL-AID is a new tool that uses denoising diffusion probabilistic models to downscale global climate data to a 0.25-degree resolution, producing high-resolution fields for temperature, wind, and precipitation with uncertainty quantification. This work addresses the critical need for high-resolution climate projections to inform adaptation and mitigation strategies, as conventional global climate models operate at 150-200 km resolution and miss essential regional processes. The model is trained on ERA5 reanalysis data and accurately reconstructs statistical distributions including extreme events, power spectra, and spatial structures, while also modeling probability distributions for uncertainty quantification.

## [AI Code Generation Bottleneck: Knowledge Externalization in Quantum Many-Body](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04089) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies knowledge externalization as a critical bottleneck in AI-assisted scientific code generation and proposes a multi-stage human-in-the-loop workflow that significantly improves success rates on quantum many-body tasks, achieving 11/26 audited passes on a Pfaffian conversion task where direct prompting yields none. This work addresses a fundamental gap in AI for science by formalizing the tacit knowledge problem in paper-to-program translation, providing a practical protocol that can make scientific code generation more reliable and auditable, which is crucial for reproducibility in computational physics. The workflow was evaluated on two tasks: DMRG from Schollwöck's review (16/16 passes with specification vs. 6/13 direct) and Pfaffian conversion from Jin et al. (11/26 audited passes vs. 0 direct). Cross-specification transfer was asymmetric: non-GPT specifications implemented by GPT-5.5 passed 4/4, while GPT-5.5 specifications implemented by weaker models failed 4/4.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum many-body algorithms like DMRG (density matrix renormalization group) and Pfaffian conversion of HFB (Hartree-Fock-Bogoliubov) states to MPS (matrix product states) are complex and rely on tacit conventions (e.g., index choices, gauges, fermionic signs) that are rarely explicit in papers. Large language models can generate code from equations but fail when correctness depends on such unstated knowledge. Knowledge externalization is the process of making this tacit expertise explicit in a structured specification before code generation.

## [WebSP-Eval: New Benchmark for Web Agent Security & Privacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06367) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced WebSP-Eval, the first evaluation framework specifically designed to measure web agent performance on website security and privacy tasks, including cookie management, privacy settings, and session revocation. The benchmark includes 200 manually crafted task instances across 28 websites and an automated evaluator. This benchmark fills a critical gap in web agent evaluation, as existing benchmarks focus on general-purpose tasks or safety against malicious actions but ignore user-facing security and privacy tasks. The findings reveal that current state-of-the-art models struggle with these tasks, particularly with stateful UI elements like toggles, highlighting a significant weakness in autonomous web agents. The evaluation tested 8 web agent instantiations using state-of-the-art multimodal large language models and found that stateful UI elements, particularly toggles, caused over 45% task failure across many models. The framework includes a custom Google Chrome extension for account and initial state management to ensure reproducible evaluations.

Existing benchmarks such as WebArena evaluate general performance, while SafeArena tests safety against malicious actions, but none assess an agent's ability to handle user-facing security and privacy tasks like managing cookie preferences or revoking sessions. WebSP-Eval addresses this gap by providing a dedicated dataset and evaluation framework.

## [Decoder Bottleneck Delays Grokking in Arithmetic Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13082) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study on arXiv reveals that grokking in encoder-decoder transformers trained on Collatz prediction is delayed because the decoder fails to access already learned structure, not because the structure is missing. This work provides causal evidence that the decoder is the bottleneck in grokking, offering a mechanistic explanation for a long-standing puzzle in transformer training and suggesting ways to accelerate generalization. Transplanting a trained encoder into a fresh model accelerates grokking by 2.75×, while freezing the encoder and retraining only the decoder eliminates the plateau, achieving 97.6% accuracy versus 86.1% for joint training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Grokking is a phenomenon where neural networks suddenly generalize long after overfitting the training data. Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural network computations. The Collatz conjecture is a famous unsolved problem in mathematics; predicting the next step in the Collatz sequence is a simple algorithmic task used to study grokking.

## [Jailbreak Vulnerability Localized to Token-Level SAE Features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23130) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a token-driven pipeline using sparse autoencoders (SAEs) to identify fine-grained feature subgroups responsible for jailbreak vulnerability in LLMs, specifically applied to Gemma-2-2B. This work advances AI safety by providing a more precise method to localize jailbreak vulnerabilities at the feature subgroup level, potentially enabling more targeted defenses without broad model modifications. The pipeline uses single-category unsafe examples from BeaverTails to reduce cross-category interference, and applies three feature-grouping strategies: cluster-based, hierarchical-linkage, and single-token-driven. Single-token-driven grouping achieves harmfulness comparable to full cluster-based grouping, showing that individual harmful prompt tokens are sufficient to localize vulnerability-relevant SAE feature subgroups.

## [Temporal Operator Attention Overcomes Softmax Bottleneck](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11287) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Temporal Operator Attention (TOA), which augments standard softmax attention with learnable sequence-space operators to enable signed and oscillatory transformations for time series analysis. TOA addresses a fundamental limitation of softmax attention—its simplex-constrained mixing bottleneck—which has hindered transformer performance in time series tasks. This could lead to more accurate forecasting, anomaly detection, and classification across domains like finance, IoT, and climate science. TOA introduces dense N×N operators but uses Stochastic Operator Regularization, a high-variance dropout mechanism, to stabilize training and prevent memorization. It consistently improves performance when integrated into backbones like PatchTST and iTransformer, especially on reconstruction-heavy tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Standard softmax attention computes outputs as convex combinations of inputs, which limits its ability to represent signed and oscillatory transformations common in time series. This is formalized as a simplex-constrained mixing bottleneck. TOA overcomes this by learning explicit operators that allow direct signed mixing across time while preserving input-dependent adaptivity.

The formal language and theorem bank enable verifiable, human-readable reasoning, advancing AI's capability in mathematical problem solving and educational applications. The method also achieves 80.2% accuracy on the plane geometry dataset PlaneFGeo3k, demonstrating its generality. Code and datasets are publicly available on GitHub.

## [PatchWorld: Gradient-Free Executable World Models via Code Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30880) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PatchWorld introduces a gradient-free framework that converts offline trajectories into executable Python world models using counterexample-guided code repair, achieving 76.4% macro success in one-step lookahead planning without any LLM calls inside the prediction module. This work bridges the gap between black-box world models and interpretable, executable code, enabling transparent planning in partially observable environments. It reduces reliance on large language models for planning, potentially lowering computational costs and improving trust in AI agents. PatchWorld uses a Counterexample Guided Inductive Synthesis (CEGIS) loop to iteratively repair Python programs representing belief-state updates. The framework also reveals a tradeoff: improving observation fidelity via residual-memory bias can weaken action-discriminative dynamics, and vice versa.

Traditional world models predict next observations using black-box neural networks, which are hard to interpret and debug. PatchWorld instead induces symbolic belief-state programs that are executable and inspectable, enabling gradient-free optimization through code repair.

## [Agent Metadata Leaks Workflow Integrity Beyond Privacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07150) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper identifies that communication-graph metadata in agent interoperability protocols like A2A and MCP poses a workflow integrity threat, not just a privacy risk, by allowing an observer to recognize and preempt pending workflows from their opening patterns. This matters because as autonomous agent systems become widespread, metadata leaks could enable adversaries to sabotage or front-run workflows across trust domains, undermining the reliability of multi-agent collaborations. The paper shows a label-blind classifier can recover a task's class from passive metadata at 6x chance, and from only the workflow's opening; a defense-aware adversary does not overturn this, and only full transport-layer protection drives recovery toward chance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Agent interoperability protocols like Google's A2A and Anthropic's MCP standardize how autonomous AI agents communicate, but they assume address-based transport (e.g., HTTP) that exposes the communication graph (who contacts whom, when, how often). SLIM is a transport binding that adds content protection via MLS, but the paper argues that even with content encryption, the graph metadata remains exposed and exploitable.

## [ResearchClawBench: Benchmark for Autonomous Scientific Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07591) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ResearchClawBench introduces a benchmark of 40 tasks across 10 scientific domains to evaluate end-to-end autonomous scientific research by AI agents, with each task grounded in a real published paper. The evaluation reveals that the best AI agent, Claude Code, achieves only 21.5% of the target paper-level re-discovery, highlighting a significant gap. This benchmark provides a standardized, reproducible way to measure progress toward autonomous scientific research, which could accelerate discovery across fields. The low scores indicate that current AI agents are far from reliable, guiding future research efforts. The benchmark uses expert-curated multimodal rubrics to decompose target scientific artifacts into weighted criteria, allowing evaluation of re-discovery while leaving room for new discovery. Error analysis shows failures concentrate in experimental protocol mismatch, evidence mismatch, and missing scientific core.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous scientific research agents aim to conduct the entire scientific process—from reading raw data to producing publication-quality results—without human intervention. Existing benchmarks often focus on isolated tasks like code generation or data analysis, but ResearchClawBench evaluates the full end-to-end pipeline. The benchmark includes a lightweight evaluation harness called ResearchHarness for testing LLMs directly.

## [Bag of Dims: Training-Free Mechanistic Interpretability via Sign Patterns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12629) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'Bag of Dims', a training-free framework that interprets transformer hidden states by treating each dimension's sign (+/-1) as a binary register, enabling feature discovery without learned rotations or optimization. This work challenges the prevailing view that interpretability requires learned rotations, showing that the standard basis alone suffices for high-accuracy feature detection across language, vision, and audio models, potentially simplifying and democratizing mechanistic interpretability research. Sign-only patterns preserve 60-93% top-5 next-token accuracy through the LM head, and decoder-free Hamming scoring achieves 80-90% top-4096 coverage. The method detects 175 categories at AUC 0.97-0.99 from a single-token cache, and flipping feature signs during forward pass suppresses the corresponding concept.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks by understanding internal computations. Traditional approaches often rely on learned rotations or probes to extract features from hidden states, which can be computationally expensive and architecture-specific. The Bag of Dims framework proposes that the standard basis of transformer hidden states already provides a meaningful feature basis without any training.

## [CineOrchestra Unifies Cinematic Video Generation Controls](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13768) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CineOrchestra is a unified video diffusion model that simultaneously controls subjects, events, cameras, and shot transitions for cinematic video generation, outperforming per-axis specialists on two new benchmarks. This is the first framework to jointly integrate multi-subject personalization, temporal control, multi-shot synthesis, and camera control, enabling fine-grained cinematic video generation from a single model. It advances AI video generation toward professional-level production capabilities. The model uses entity-centric conditioning primitives with a shared structure, solved via two parameter-free coordinated rotary embeddings: interval-sampled temporal RoPE and 2D entity-temporal cross-attention RoPE. It achieves consistent attention across events of varying duration and disambiguates per-entity conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Cinematic video generation requires controlling multiple subjects, events, camera movements, and shot transitions simultaneously. Previous text-to-video models addressed each aspect separately, lacking a unified approach. Diffusion models are a class of generative models that iteratively denoise data to produce high-quality outputs, and video diffusion transformers (DiT) extend this to video generation.

## [Unified 2x2 Framework for BCI Sensory Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15091) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a unified 2x2 framework categorizing brain-computer interfaces (BCIs) for sensory restoration along two axes: invasiveness (invasive vs. non-invasive) and signal direction (afferent sensory-IN vs. efferent sensory-OUT). It also defines and distinguishes the paradigms of restoration, substitution, and augmentation, and outlines a convergence roadmap. This framework addresses the fragmentation in BCI sensory restoration research by providing consistent terminology and comparison metrics, which could accelerate progress in neuroprosthetics. It also highlights the integrative role of machine learning foundation models, potentially guiding future research and development. The framework's two axes—invasiveness and signal direction—yield four quadrants: invasive afferent, invasive efferent, non-invasive afferent, and non-invasive efferent. The paper also provides a structural roadmap for convergence over near-, medium-, and long-term horizons, focusing on physical limits and the role of foundation models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Brain-computer interfaces (BCIs) enable direct communication between the brain and external devices, offering potential for sensory and motor restoration. However, the field is fragmented between invasive neuroprosthetics (requiring surgery) and non-invasive electrophysiological decoders (using scalp electrodes). Afferent signals carry sensory information to the brain, while efferent signals carry motor commands from the brain. The terms restoration, substitution, and augmentation are often used interchangeably, causing confusion.

## [CVP: Efficient Uncertainty Estimation for Bayesian Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16214) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Calibrated Variance Propagation (CVP), a single-pass method for uncertainty estimation in Bayesian deep learning that extends variance propagation to modern architectures including transformers and CNNs with normalization layers. CVP addresses the prohibitive test-time cost of Monte Carlo sampling in Bayesian deep learning, enabling reliable uncertainty estimates at a fraction of the computational cost, which is critical for high-stakes applications like medical diagnosis and autonomous driving. CVP introduces a new propagation method for normalization layers, combines it with existing techniques for activation functions, and uses a light calibration step to absorb residual errors. It improves coverage at 0.5% risk from 8.2% to 14.6% with BEiT-3 on NLVR2 and from 2.6% to 10.8% with ViLT on VQAv2.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian deep learning aims to learn a distribution over model parameters to quantify uncertainty, but test-time inference requires averaging predictions over many forward passes with sampled weights, which is computationally expensive. Variance propagation computes analytical uncertainty estimates in a single forward pass, but prior work was limited to simple architectures like MLPs. CVP extends this to modern deep networks with normalization layers and complex activation functions.

Understanding these alternatives helps developers avoid cluttering every repository with IDE or OS-specific files and reduces noise in diffs for auto-generated files like package-lock.json. Global excludes are configured via `git config --global core.excludesFile ~/.config/git/ignore`, and .gitattributes can mark files as binary to suppress diffs, e.g., `package-lock.json binary`.

## [SGCD: Skill-Guided Distillation for GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18890) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Skill-Guided Continuation Distillation (SGCD), an iterative self-improvement framework that provides supervision for off-trajectory states in GUI agent training by mixing expert trajectories with continuations from a skill-guided policy. This addresses a critical limitation in GUI agent training—policy-induced off-trajectory states—and demonstrates significant performance gains, improving success rates from the low-30% range to over 50% on OSWorld-Verified, which could accelerate the development of more robust GUI agents. SGCD first runs the plain policy without skill guidance to reach realistic off-trajectory states, then uses a skill-guided policy to complete the task and produce successful continuations. Skills are extracted from both successful and failed rollouts, including Continuation Plans, Critical Targets, Failure Traps, and Success Criteria.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: GUI agents are AI systems that interact with graphical user interfaces by perceiving screenshots and performing actions like clicks and typing. They are typically trained via behavior cloning on expert trajectories, but when the agent's policy deviates from the expert, it encounters off-trajectory states where no supervision is available, leading to poor generalization.
