## [ThinkDeception: Interpretable Multimodal Deception Detection via RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18988) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ThinkDeception introduces a progressive reinforcement learning framework that leverages multimodal large language models and chain-of-thought reasoning to detect deception with interpretable rationales, achieving state-of-the-art performance on mainstream benchmarks. This work addresses the critical lack of interpretability in existing black-box deception detection systems, making it possible to understand why a statement is deemed deceptive. It has significant implications for security, forensic analysis, and AI trustworthiness. The framework includes a novel Visual-Audio Consistency Group Relative Policy Optimization (VAC-GRPO) with a progressive training strategy that stratifies data into four difficulty tiers. ## [ARIADNE: Training-Free Dynamic Adapter Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19079) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ARIADNE introduces a training-free, adapter-agnostic framework that selects the best adapter for each inference-time query by comparing input embeddings to centroids computed from each adapter's training data. This approach enables scalable and portable adapter selection in deployed AI systems without requiring access to adapter internals or additional training, addressing a key bottleneck in managing large adapter pools. Evaluated on Llama 3.2 1B Instruct with 23 NLP tasks, ARIADNE recovers 97.44% of the upper bound performance; scaling to 44 tasks, it achieves 89.7% average selection accuracy without any training or adapter modification.

**Background**: Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) methods like LoRA add small adapters to a frozen backbone model, enabling task specialization without full retraining. In multi-task deployments, selecting the correct adapter for an unlabeled input is challenging; existing methods often require adapter-specific knowledge or additional training, limiting scalability.

## [QSignAI: Quantum-Randomness-Seeded Identity Signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27729) ⭐️ 7.0/10

QSignAI is a production-deployed platform that integrates quantum-randomness-seeded identity signatures with an AI conversational bot for real-time event participation, using a Toeplitz two-source extractor over independent single-qubit Hadamard measurements on SV1 and DM1 simulators plus a 2-qubit Bell state. This work bridges the 2024-2025 Nobel and Turing-recognized fields of AI and quantum science in a deployed system, making quantum phenomena perceptually legible to general audiences through an AI bot. It demonstrates a practical bidirectional AI-quantum relationship that could inspire future hybrid applications. The current deployment uses cloud quantum simulators (SV1 and DM1 on Amazon Braket), with physical QPU randomness identified as a near-term extension. Measurable benchmarks are prioritized as future work, and the paper provides qualitative deployment evidence from live events.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum randomness generation uses the inherent unpredictability of quantum measurements to produce truly random numbers, unlike classical pseudo-random generators. A Toeplitz two-source extractor is a post-processing method that combines two independent weak random sources to produce a nearly perfect random output, often used in quantum random number generators. SV1 and DM1 are cloud-based quantum simulators provided by Amazon Braket, where SV1 simulates pure state vectors and DM1 simulates density matrices for mixed states.

## [IOAH3: Adaptive Spatial Partitioning to Solve MAUP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18280) ⭐️ 7.0/10

IOAH3 is a new method that constructs adaptive spatial partitions by extracting multi-source features (road density, POI density, building density, terrain roughness) and using importance scoring via PCA, followed by Markov Random Field graph-cut optimization and hierarchical refinement at finer H3 resolutions. This addresses the modifiable areal unit problem (MAUP), a fundamental bias in spatial analysis where results depend on arbitrary aggregation boundaries. IOAH3 enables data-driven, adaptive partitions that preserve fine-scale structure, benefiting GIS, urban planning, and spatial data science. The method uses H3 hexagonal grids as the base spatial index and incorporates population and flood-hazard data as auxiliary inputs for cell filtering and spatial smoothness. The hierarchical refinement step propagates support from neighboring cells to avoid isolated fine-resolution islands.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: The modifiable areal unit problem (MAUP) is a statistical bias that occurs when point-based spatial data are aggregated into arbitrary areal units (e.g., census tracts, administrative boundaries), affecting results like population density or illness rates. H3 is a discrete global grid system developed by Uber that indexes geographic space into hierarchical hexagonal cells. Adaptive spatial partitioning aims to create data-driven zones that reflect underlying data patterns rather than fixed boundaries.

This addresses the critical retain-forget trade-off in LLM unlearning, offering a practical, source-agnostic fix that can be applied to any existing unlearning method, potentially improving privacy and safety without sacrificing model utility. CAOA introduces a method that integrates point cloud completion with symmetry-aware pose estimation to align CAD models to objects in RGB-D scans, achieving a 17% accuracy improvement on the Scan2CAD benchmark over state-of-the-art methods. This work addresses the critical challenge of aligning CAD models to real-world scans, which is essential for 3D semantic reconstruction and robotics applications. By bridging the synthetic-to-real domain gap with a tailored data generation strategy, CAOA improves the practicality of CAD alignment in real-world scenarios. CAOA uses a semantically and contextually aware point cloud completion module and a symmetry-aware loss to handle symmetric ambiguities. The authors also release S2C-Completion, an expert-annotated dataset of over 8,500 object-CAD pairs from Scan2CAD, as a new benchmark for real-world single-object completion.

## [Distance-Adaptive Representation Reduces KV Cache Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18587) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper introduces Distance-Adaptive Representation (DAR), which reduces the dimensionality of key and value representations for distant tokens in decoder-only Transformers, cutting KV cache memory while maintaining performance. This challenges the uniform dimensionality assumption for KV cache, offering a practical way to reduce memory usage in large language models during inference, which is a major bottleneck for scaling context lengths. DAR preserves full-dimensional representations within a local context window and uses reduced dimensions (e.g., 1/4) for tokens beyond that window, tested across models from 70M to 410M parameters and fine-tuning on a 1B-scale model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Decoder-only Transformers, like GPT, use a KV cache to store key and value vectors for all previous tokens, which grows linearly with sequence length and becomes a memory bottleneck. Traditionally, all tokens use the same dimensionality for keys and values, but this paper hypothesizes that distant tokens need less representational capacity.

Deep learning methods can learn robust features but often fail to generalize to unseen conditions. NeuralMUSIC combines the strengths of both by using a neural network to estimate a cleaner covariance matrix for MUSIC.

## [SWE-Future: Forecast-Conditioned Data Synthesis for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SWE-Future proposes a forecast-conditioned data synthesis method that generates future-oriented coding tasks using only pre-forecast repository evidence, avoiding benchmark contamination. In an 80-repository study, the forecaster achieved 58.1% future-work relevance, and a 200-task dataset was synthesized across 61 repositories. This method addresses the critical issue of benchmark contamination in software engineering AI agents, which undermines the credibility of performance evaluations. By generating realistic, future-oriented tasks, SWE-Future enables more reliable assessment and development of coding agents. The method uses a forecast snapshot at time T0 and only pre-T0 repository evidence to predict task families (feature implementation, bugfix, refactor). Later pull requests are used only for validation, not for task generation, ensuring no overlap with training data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Benchmark contamination occurs when AI models are evaluated on tasks that overlap with their training data, inflating performance scores. Traditional coding benchmarks often replay historical GitHub issues and pull requests, which may have been seen during pretraining. SWE-Future avoids this by synthesizing tasks based on predicted future work, not past events.

## [Cond-DP: Faster Private Regression via Public Features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18773) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Cond-DP, a variant of DPSGD that conditions on public features to accelerate convergence in differentially private regression, with theoretical guarantees for convex, strongly convex, and non-convex settings. This work addresses an underexplored area—private regression with public features—which is common in recommendation and advertising systems, and offers provably faster convergence without additional privacy cost. Cond-DP constructs a data-driven conditioning matrix from public features to reshape the optimization landscape, and recovers standard DPSGD when the matrix is the identity. Empirically, it consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines across various datasets and model architectures under label DP.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Differential privacy (DP) is a framework that bounds the influence of any single data point on algorithm output, often achieved via DP-SGD which clips and adds noise to gradients. In many real-world applications, features are split into public (non-sensitive) and private (sensitive) parts; existing work focuses on classification, leaving regression less explored. Cond-DP leverages the rapidly decaying spectra of public feature matrices to improve optimization efficiency.

## [SHIFT: Training-Free Method to Reduce Language Bias in Multilingual Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18801) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose SHIFT, a training-free feature transformation method that corrects language-specific offsets in document embeddings during indexing to mitigate language bias in multilingual dense retrieval. Language bias is a critical issue in multilingual information retrieval, often causing same-language documents to dominate results. SHIFT offers a practical, training-free solution that can be applied to existing dense retrieval models, potentially improving cross-language search quality in real-world systems. SHIFT uses parallel translation pairs to estimate a relative language vector for each target language relative to a source language, then subtracts this vector from document embeddings during indexing. Evaluations on four MLIR benchmarks show it effectively reduces language bias and improves retrieval performance across diverse dense retrieval models.

**Background**: Multilingual dense retrieval uses neural models to encode queries and documents into a shared vector space, enabling cross-language search. However, these models often exhibit language bias, where documents in the same language as the query are ranked higher even if other-language documents are more relevant. SHIFT addresses this by adjusting embeddings at indexing time without additional training.

## [Rescaling MLM-Head for Neural Sparse Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18811) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper identifies that using stronger pretrained encoders in SPLADE can cause performance degradation due to scale mismatch in the MLM head, and proposes a simple initialization-time correction that rescales the MLM-head projection by a constant factor. This finding is significant because it reveals that the bottleneck in adapting pretrained encoders to learned sparse retrieval is not encoder capacity alone but the calibration of the MLM-head scale, and the zero-cost correction can turn unstable training runs into competitive sparse retrievers. The correction is applied at initialization time without modifying the model architecture or training objective, and it substantially improves large-norm backbones such as ModernBERT and Ettin across both in-domain and out-of-domain retrieval benchmarks.

**Background**: SPLADE is a neural sparse retrieval model that uses the BERT MLM head to produce sparse lexical representations, and query-document relevance is computed via an unnormalized dot product. TRUST is a novel framework that generates counterfactual explanations by directly incorporating user-specified confidence targets using Probabilistic Tsetlin Machines (PTMs) and Bayesian optimization, rather than merely minimizing input changes. This addresses a key limitation of existing counterfactual methods that produce fragile recourse near decision boundaries, enabling more robust and reliable algorithmic recourse in high-stakes domains like finance and healthcare. TRUST achieves perfect robustness on multiple benchmarks while maintaining low recourse cost, e.g., an L2 distance of 0.10 on the Haberman dataset at 0.92 confidence, and it reveals rule-level stability through PTM's probabilistic clause structure.

## [CAPRA: Multi-Agent LLM System for Architecture Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18976) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CAPRA is a multi-agent LLM system that automates personalized feedback on software architecture deliverables by parsing text and UML diagrams from student reports. It uses PyMuPDF and vision-enabled GPT-4o for multi-modal extraction and a deterministic Evidence Anchoring step to reduce hallucinations. This addresses a gap in automated assessment for software architecture education, where structural completeness and requirements traceability are hard to evaluate automatically. CAPRA's 88.8% criterion satisfaction rate and moderate agreement with human evaluators suggest LLM-supported architectural feedback is viable, potentially reducing instructor workload. CAPRA coordinates multiple specialized agents and uses a Python microservice with PyMuPDF and GPT-4o for document extraction. It introduces a ConsistencyManager agent for cross-verification and uses normalized Levenshtein distance for fuzzy matching to anchor evidence. In a preliminary evaluation on 10 reports, it processed each in about 4 minutes.

Researchers propose CADE, a framework for time-series question answering that uses direct timestep embedding and one-directional supervised contrastive alignment to overcome the tokenization bottleneck of large language models. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in applying LLMs to time-series data, enabling more accurate and flexible question answering across diverse tasks without requiring patching or dataset-specific tuning. CADE maps each timestep directly into the LLM embedding space via a point-wise linear encoder and MLP projector, preserving exact index-level access, and uses a novel one-directional supervised contrastive loss to align time-series embeddings with frozen class-name text anchors.

Researchers introduced G-IdiomAlign, a gloss-pivoted benchmark for evaluating how well large language models align idioms across languages, revealing that LLMs often default to literal translations and that adding glosses yields only modest improvements. Idiom translation is a persistent challenge in cross-lingual NLP, and this benchmark provides a standardized way to measure and diagnose failures, highlighting the need for better semantic understanding in LLMs. The benchmark uses English glosses from Wiktionary as pivots and supports two evaluation protocols: multiple-choice idiom equivalence with typed distractors, and gloss-contrastive generation that compares outputs with and without glosses. Analysis on Qwen3-8B shows that cross-condition differences are more concentrated in attention heads than in layers.

## [PQLRM: Combining Pareto Q-Learning and Reward Machines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19134) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PQLRM is a new multi-objective reinforcement learning algorithm that integrates Pareto Q-Learning with Reward Machines to efficiently approximate Pareto-optimal policies under non-Markovian rewards. This work addresses the challenge of multi-objective RL with non-Markovian rewards, which is common in real-world tasks, and offers a sample-efficient method that outperforms naive baselines. PQLRM maintains sets of vector-valued Q-estimates to approximate the Pareto front and exploits the factored automaton structure of reward machines. Experiments show it converges faster than a naive PQL baseline on the cross-product MDP and can synthesize policies that QRM cannot.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-objective reinforcement learning (MORL) deals with problems where an agent must optimize multiple conflicting objectives. Pareto Q-Learning (PQL) is a MORL algorithm that maintains a set of Q-values to approximate the Pareto front. Reward Machines (RMs) are finite-state machines that encode reward functions with structure, enabling faster learning by exploiting that structure. Non-Markovian rewards depend on the history of states rather than just the current state, making them harder to handle.

## [OneCanvas: 3D Scene Understanding via Panoramic Reprojection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19253) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OneCanvas introduces a method that aggregates multi-view patch features onto a single equirectangular panoramic canvas for 3D scene understanding, eliminating the need for complex geometry encoders. It achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on SQA3D and VSI-Bench with an order of magnitude less training compute than competing methods. This work simplifies 3D scene understanding in Vision-Language Models (VLMs) by avoiding complex geometry encoders and large training budgets, making spatial reasoning more efficient. It directly supports situated reasoning from a specific viewpoint, which is crucial for robotics and embodied AI applications. Each patch is unprojected to a 3D world coordinate using depth and camera pose, then placed on the canvas at the corresponding longitude and latitude without rasterization or aggregation. A 3D position embedding of the metric coordinates is added to restore depth information lost during projection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Existing approaches to 3D scene understanding in VLMs often rely on complex geometry encoders or large training datasets. OneCanvas uses an equirectangular projection, a common technique for creating panoramic images, to map all views onto a single canvas. This allows the VLM to process the representation as a regular image, simplifying the architecture.

## [Structured Cognitive Loop Boosts LLM Agent Accountability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05107) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced the Structured Cognitive Loop (SCL), a modular architecture for LLM agents that separates cognition, memory, control, and action, achieving 86.3% task success across 360 episodes compared to 70.5-76.8% for ReAct and LangChain baselines. SCL addresses a critical gap in AI safety and interpretability by enforcing epistemic accountability through a lightweight controller that checks preconditions and authorizes actions, making agent decisions inspectable and auditable. The architecture includes a controller that prevents redundant actions and authorizes tool use, and subsequent extensions integrate Human-in-the-Loop control, Pool Gated Retrieval, and the Horizon Warrant Commitment framework.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Current LLM agents like ReAct combine reasoning and acting in a single prompt, making it hard to verify why an action was taken or where errors occurred. SCL modularizes these functions to improve transparency and accountability.

<a id="item-228"></a>
## [Robust Regularized Policy Iteration for Offline RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09344) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Robust Regularized Policy Iteration (RRPI), a novel offline RL algorithm that replaces the intractable max-min robust objective with a tractable KL-regularized surrogate and derives an efficient policy iteration procedure with a robust regularized Bellman operator. The method achieves strong performance on D4RL benchmarks, outperforming recent baselines on most environments. RRPI provides a unified framework to address distribution shift and transition uncertainty in offline RL, which are critical challenges that limit real-world deployment. Its theoretical guarantees (γ-contraction and monotonic improvement) and strong empirical results make it a promising approach for safe and data-efficient policy learning. The robust regularized Bellman operator is proven to be a γ-contraction, and iterative updates of the surrogate yield monotonic improvement of the original robust objective. RRPI aligns lower Q-values with high epistemic uncertainty, preventing unreliable out-of-distribution actions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning learns policies from static datasets without online interaction, but suffers from distribution shift when the learned policy encounters unseen state-action pairs. Robust optimization treats the transition kernel as a decision variable within an uncertainty set to handle model uncertainty. KL-regularized RL adds a KL divergence penalty to align the policy with a reference, promoting stability and sample efficiency.

## [Distributionally Robust RL for Urban EV Fleet Dispatch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25848) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose PD-RSAC, a distributionally robust reinforcement learning framework for city-scale EV ride-hailing fleet dispatch that enforces physical constraints via a rolling mixed-integer linear program (MILP) projection. This work addresses the critical challenge of ensuring physical feasibility under distributional shifts in urban EV dispatch, achieving higher net profit ($1.22M) than strong baselines while maintaining zero feeder-limit violations, which could improve real-world EV fleet efficiency and grid stability. The framework uses a Wasserstein-1 ambiguity set with a graph-aligned Mahalanobis ground metric to capture spatial correlations, and optimizes a Soft Actor-Critic (SAC) agent with a primal-dual risk-budget update. Experiments on a simulator built from NYC taxi data show PD-RSAC outperforms Greedy, SAC, MAPPO, and MADDPG baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Distributionally robust reinforcement learning (DRRL) optimizes policies for worst-case performance within an uncertainty set around a nominal model, addressing the sim-to-real gap. A semi-Markov decision process (semi-MDP) generalizes MDPs by allowing variable action durations, suitable for EV dispatch where charging and repositioning take variable time. The Wasserstein-1 ambiguity set defines a ball of probability measures around a nominal distribution using Wasserstein distance, enabling robust optimization.

SkillRevise retrieves repair principles from a general memory and applies execution-anchored edits, re-executing candidates and retaining the first verifier-passing skill within a revision budget.

Researchers propose quality-aware self-distillation (QASD) for VLM-based GUI grounding, which uses soft correctness-aware gating and teacher-probability scaling to improve coordinate-token teacher signals. This method addresses a key limitation of on-policy self-distillation in GUI grounding, where teacher signals degrade under student-generated prefixes, and consistently improves performance across six benchmarks. Neither component alone improves performance, but their combination does.

## [AI News Perceived Similarly, Disclosure Boosts Engagement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03500) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A preregistered survey experiment with 599 participants in German-speaking Switzerland found that readers perceive AI-generated, AI-assisted, and human-written news excerpts as similar in quality. After disclosure of AI involvement, participants reported higher willingness to continue reading the article, but future reading intentions for AI-generated news remained unchanged. This study provides empirical evidence that AI-generated news can be as credible as human-written news, alleviating concerns about quality degradation. The finding that disclosure momentarily increases engagement suggests news organizations can transparently label AI use without harming reader interest, which has practical implications for journalism ethics and AI adoption. The experiment used two short news excerpts and measured perceived credibility, readability, and expertise before disclosure. After disclosure, participants in AI-assisted and AI-generated conditions reported higher willingness to continue reading compared to the control group, but no difference was found in future willingness to read AI-generated news across conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Automated journalism uses AI to produce news content, raising questions about audience trust and quality perception. Previous research has shown mixed results: some studies find AI-generated news is trusted less, while others find no significant difference. This experiment adds to the literature by examining both quality perceptions and engagement intentions after disclosure.

## [Generalized KL Divergence Loss Improves Optimization and Reduces Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08038) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper mathematically proves that the Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence loss is equivalent to a decoupled form (DKL) consisting of weighted MSE and cross-entropy with soft labels, and proposes a Generalized KL (GKL) divergence loss that breaks asymmetric optimization and incorporates class-wise global information to reduce bias. The GKL loss achieves state-of-the-art adversarial robustness on RobustBench and competitive knowledge distillation performance across multiple datasets, offering practical improvements for training robust and efficient deep learning models. The improvements include a smoother weight function to address convergence issues in knowledge distillation, and the integration of class-wise global statistics to mitigate sample-level bias. Experiments were conducted on CIFAR-10/100, ImageNet, and vision-language datasets for adversarial training and knowledge distillation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence is a widely used loss function in machine learning for measuring the difference between two probability distributions, often employed in knowledge distillation and adversarial training. The Decoupled KL (DKL) loss decomposes KL into weighted MSE and cross-entropy terms, revealing its structure and enabling targeted improvements.

## [GRILL Exposes Hidden Vulnerabilities in Autoencoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03646) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper reveals that existing white-box attacks on autoencoders underestimate robustness due to vanishing gradients in ill-conditioned layers, and proposes GRILL (Gradient Signal Restoration in Ill-Conditioned Layers) to restore gradient signals for more reliable adversarial evaluation. This work exposes a fundamental flaw in adversarial robustness evaluation of autoencoders, which are widely used in anomaly detection and generative tasks. GRILL enables more accurate vulnerability assessment, potentially leading to stronger defenses and safer deployment of autoencoder-based systems. GRILL mitigates gradient degradation caused by near-zero singular values in intermediate weight matrices of ill-conditioned layers. Experiments across multiple autoencoder architectures show that GRILL significantly increases attack effectiveness under both sample-specific and universal attack settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Autoencoders are neural networks that learn compressed latent representations of input data, often used for dimensionality reduction or anomaly detection. Adversarial attacks craft small perturbations to input that cause large reconstruction errors, but existing attacks may fail due to vanishing gradients in certain layers. The vanishing gradient problem occurs when gradients become extremely small during backpropagation, hindering optimization. Ill-conditioned layers have weight matrices with a high condition number, making gradient propagation unstable.

## [Probing LLM Metaphor Processing: Semantic Drift and Lexical Anchors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04120) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study introduces a diagnostic framework probing three dimensions of LLM metaphor processing: semantic attribute alignment, lexical invariance, and syntactic sensitivity. It reveals that LLM-generated interpretations can exhibit semantic drift, stable lexical anchors persist across contexts, and detection performance is sensitive to syntactic irregularities. These findings challenge the assumption that strong behavioral performance on metaphor tasks reflects robust semantic understanding, highlighting the need for caution when interpreting metaphor benchmarks. This work provides a more nuanced view of how LLMs process figurative language, which is critical for improving model interpretability and reliability. The framework uses geometric probing for semantic alignment, context-varying substitution for lexical invariance, and controlled syntactic perturbations for syntactic sensitivity. Results show that lexical anchors may support conventional metaphors but bias novel ones requiring contextual integration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Metaphor processing is a key aspect of natural language understanding, requiring models to grasp figurative meanings beyond literal semantics. Large language models (LLMs) like GPT-4 have shown strong performance on metaphor detection and interpretation benchmarks, but it remains unclear whether this reflects genuine understanding or reliance on surface-level patterns. This study probes three complementary dimensions to disentangle the underlying mechanisms.

## [Rethinking Cross-lingual Gaps via Statistical Variance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15551) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper formalizes cross-lingual gaps in LLMs as biased and unbiased errors, and demonstrates that controlling response variance through test-time ensemble methods reduces the gap by up to 12 absolute points. This provides a new statistical framework for understanding and mitigating cross-lingual performance disparities, which is critical for deploying LLMs equitably across languages. The authors propose multiple test-time ensemble methods that reduce response variance, achieving relative gains of 8% to over 50% across various LLMs. The work is the first to formalize cross-lingual gaps in terms of biased and unbiased errors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Cross-lingual gaps refer to performance drops when LLMs are queried in a target language compared to the source language used during training. ## [Posterior Continuation for Diffusion Inverse Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00176) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new posterior continuation framework for diffusion inverse problems is proposed, which adapts measurement guidance to noise levels by exposing frequencies gradually, achieving up to 5 dB PSNR improvement on motion deblurring. This work addresses a key limitation in diffusion posterior sampling—unreliable full-band guidance at high noise levels—and provides a principled solution that improves restoration quality across multiple inverse problems, potentially advancing applications in image reconstruction and beyond. The framework constructs a family of intermediate posteriors that emphasize reliable frequency bands based on noise level, and instantiates it with a stabilized sampler combining a diffusion predictor, frequency-limited likelihood refinement, and a Haar-domain commitment rule.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate data by reversing a noise process, and can solve inverse problems (e.g., super-resolution) by combining a pretrained diffusion prior with measurement guidance. However, at high noise levels, the guidance can be unreliable because the score estimate contains errors and high-frequency measurement directions are hard to identify. The proposed method addresses this by gradually exposing measurement frequencies according to the noise level.

Researchers propose UNO, a unified framework that effectively learns from noisy user interaction logs to improve large language model systems. UNO distills logs into structured rules and preference pairs, uses clustering to manage data heterogeneity, and adaptively filters noise to enhance future responses. This addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM deployment: learning from real-world user feedback despite noisy and unstructured logs. UNO outperforms RAG and memory-based baselines, offering a practical path for continual model improvement without relying solely on expensive high-quality data. UNO first quantifies the cognitive gap between the model's prior knowledge and log data to guide adaptive noise filtering. It then constructs separate modules for primary and reflective experiences extracted from logs, achieving state-of-the-art effectiveness and efficiency in experiments.

The off-policy optimization problem further complicates learning, as the data distribution differs from the model's current policy. UNO tackles these challenges by combining rule distillation, clustering, and adaptive filtering.

In a 1-in-5 selection task, MemRerank achieves up to +10.61 absolute accuracy improvement over baselines. This work addresses a key limitation in LLM-based shopping agents: effectively using long purchase histories for personalization without noise or length issues. MemRerank provides a practical memory module that can be integrated into e-commerce systems, potentially improving user experience and conversion rates. The memory extractor is trained via reinforcement learning using downstream reranking performance as supervision, making the memory directly optimized for the reranking task. The framework is evaluated on an end-to-end benchmark with two LLM-based rerankers, consistently outperforming no-memory, raw-history, and off-the-shelf memory baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based shopping agents often rely on user purchase history for personalization, but simply appending raw history to prompts is ineffective due to noise, length, and relevance mismatch. Product reranking is a common step in e-commerce search where initial results are reordered based on deeper semantic understanding. MemRerank introduces explicit preference memory that is query-independent and optimized for reranking.

Clin-JEPA introduces a five-phase co-training framework for joint-embedding predictive pretraining on EHR patient trajectories, enabling a single backbone to forecast trajectories and perform diverse risk-prediction tasks without per-task fine-tuning. This work addresses a key challenge in medical AI by producing a single pretrained model that simultaneously forecasts patient trajectories and excels at downstream risk prediction, potentially reducing the need for task-specific models and improving clinical decision support. The framework uses a Qwen3-8B-based encoder and a 92M-parameter latent trajectory predictor, achieving mean AUROC of 0.851 on ICareFM EEP and 0.883 on 8 binary risk tasks, outperforming baselines by +0.038 and +0.041 respectively.

**Background**: Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) is a self-supervised learning paradigm that predicts abstract representations (embeddings) of missing or future data in latent space, rather than reconstructing raw inputs. Previous JEPA frameworks either discarded the predictor after pretraining or trained it on a frozen encoder, limiting their use for trajectory forecasting. Clin-JEPA introduces a multi-phase co-training curriculum to stably train both encoder and predictor together, avoiding representation collapse and drift.

Researchers introduced a tunable partial-SWAP operation into recurrent quantum reservoir networks, enabling direct control over memory dissipation rate. This mechanism was validated on IBM QPUs using short-term memory recall and NARMA-5 benchmarks. This work addresses a key gap in quantum reservoir computing by making memory capacity controllable, which is crucial for practical NISQ implementations. It enhances the utility of recurrent quantum architectures for real-world temporal tasks. The partial-SWAP is implemented as a controlled amplitude-damping channel on gate-based QPUs. The theory links the SWAP angle to memory dissipation, and experiments on IBM QPUs show improved performance on the NARMA-5 dataset.

The partial-SWAP gate is a quantum operation that superposes swapped and unswapped states, allowing tunable interaction.

## [DySink: Dynamic Frame Sinks for Long Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21028) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DySink proposes a retrieval-based framework that replaces static early-frame sinks with dynamic frame sinks selected from a compact memory bank, improving long-range context adaptability in autoregressive video generation. This addresses a key limitation in autoregressive long video generation where static sinks cause outdated context and sink collapse, enabling more coherent and natural long-horizon visual evolution. DySink couples adaptive retrieval with a sink anomaly gate that detects excessive inter-head consensus and suppresses collapse-prone context, and experiments on minute-long videos show improved temporal quality and dynamic degree.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive video generation models often use bounded-memory streaming with local windows for short-term continuity and static early-frame sinks as long-range anchors. However, as the video evolves, these static sinks become mismatched with the current visual state, and RoPE-induced phase re-alignment can homogenize inter-head attention, leading to sink collapse where content regresses toward sink frames.

## [Neuro-Symbolic Memory Transfer for Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22142) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a neuro-symbolic method that decides which symbolic observations to transfer from short-term to long-term memory in knowledge graphs under partial observability, using a per-item Q-learning design with shared parameters. This work addresses a critical gap in reinforcement learning under partial observability by enabling explicit, interpretable memory decisions, outperforming symbolic and neural baselines on the RoomKG benchmark, which could improve AI systems' reliability and efficiency in memory-constrained environments. The method uses a temporal-difference update over matched items across consecutive steps to handle variable-sized short-term buffers. On the RoomKG benchmark with long-term memory capacity 128, a lightweight local short-term-only variant performed best, keeping navigation- and query-relevant facts while discarding lower-value candidates.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning under partial observability requires agents to retain relevant information from past observations, but most memory-based approaches do not explicitly model the transfer of symbolic observations from short-term to long-term memory. Knowledge graphs store structured information as triples (subject, predicate, object), and neuro-symbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning to leverage both pattern recognition and explicit knowledge. The RoomKG benchmark evaluates agents on navigation and query tasks in a partially observable environment with memory constraints.

HAARES introduces a lightweight residual basis router that adds a half-split detail basis to block-level residual routing, exposing coarse intra-block trajectory information without dense sublayer-level routing. This method consistently improves performance in 48-layer transformer models across multiple benchmarks, offering a FLOP-light approach to enhance deep transformer architectures. The detail basis is computed as the difference between first-half and second-half residual updates, RMS-matched and updated online. Gains are most reliable in 48-layer models, with improvements over Block AttnRes across three seeds in the 201M setting.

## [SymQNet: Amortized RL for Low-Latency Adaptive Hamiltonian Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12808) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SymQNet introduces an amortized reinforcement learning approach that reduces acquisition latency in adaptive Hamiltonian learning by 47x to 72x compared to traditional online methods like bounded Fisher-information search and BALD. This breakthrough makes adaptive Hamiltonian learning practical for repeated low-latency workloads, which is crucial for real-time calibration and characterization of quantum devices, potentially accelerating the development of scalable quantum computers. SymQNet learns a posterior-conditioned acquisition policy offline via reinforcement learning and uses a fast forward pass online, retaining Bayesian posterior feedback. On a 12-qubit transverse-field Ising model, full simulated steps take 1.02 seconds for SymQNet versus 13.27 seconds for bounded two-step BALD.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Adaptive Hamiltonian learning is a process used to calibrate quantum devices by iteratively designing experiments based on previous outcomes. Traditional Bayesian design rules recompute the optimal next experiment after each posterior update, which can take seconds per step, leading to significant wall-clock latency over hundreds of shots. Amortized reinforcement learning shifts the computational burden to an offline training phase, enabling fast online decision-making.

The company plans to deploy 50,000 such scanners within six years and open a spa in San Francisco by late 2027 for initial deployment. This move could democratize medical imaging by making full-body scans affordable and accessible, potentially enabling early diagnosis of diseases on a global scale. The scanner uses a ring of 40 chips, each with 8,960 ultrasound elements capable of both transmitting and receiving, totaling over 358,000 elements. The patient is lowered into water and scanned as they pass through the ring, producing a 3D image in about 60 seconds. Midjourney licensed Butterfly Network's Ultrasound-on-Chip technology for $15 million upfront.

**Background**: Traditional MRI machines are expensive (often millions of dollars) and slow, limiting access to advanced imaging. Ultrasound is cheaper and faster but typically limited to small areas. Midjourney's approach uses a water-immersion system and a massive array of ultrasound-on-chip elements to scan the entire body quickly. The company's founder, David Holz, previously founded LeapMotion, a hand-tracking hardware startup.

Researchers propose ImpSH, a triplet-based framework that aligns posts with their implied statements and uses context-bounded semi-hard negative mining to improve cross-domain generalizability in implicit hate speech classification. Implicit hate speech is notoriously difficult to detect because it relies on insinuation rather than explicit slurs, and existing models often overfit to specific datasets. ImpSH's approach could lead to more robust and transferable hate speech detection systems, benefiting content moderation across different platforms and contexts. ImpSH uses a triplet loss with context-bounded semi-hard negatives, which focuses learning on near confusions rather than easy or extremely hard negatives. Evaluations on IHC, SBIC, and DynaHate datasets with BERT and HateBERT show that ImpSH often improves cross-domain performance compared to standard supervised contrastive baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Implicit hate speech detection is challenging because the hateful intent is conveyed through context, insinuation, or coded language rather than explicit hate words. Contrastive learning approaches aim to learn representations that pull similar examples together and push dissimilar ones apart, but they can overfit to superficial patterns. Semi-hard negative mining selects negative examples that are moderately difficult, helping the model learn finer distinctions without being overwhelmed by very hard negatives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: ABox abduction is a reasoning service that finds minimal sets of assertions to add to a knowledge base to make a missing entailment hold. Repair semantics handle inconsistencies by considering only maximal consistent subsets (repairs) of the knowledge base. Brave semantics accept a hypothesis if it holds in at least one repair, while AR semantics require it to hold in all repairs. EL_bot is a lightweight description logic used for ontology-based data access.

## [AI Sovereignty as National Learning Capacity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00729) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A viewpoint paper proposes using Human-Centered Learning Mechanics (HCLM) to analyze national AI development as a balance between information injection, absorptive capacity, and institutional dissipation, with a focus on France. This reframes AI policy as governance of an open, strategic, non-equilibrium learning system, potentially offering a new lens for comparing national AI strategies beyond traditional metrics like investment or compute. The paper treats regulation not as mere friction but as a potential enhancer of long-term learning capacity through legitimacy and trust. Numerical results are diagnostic scenarios, not econometric estimates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Human-Centered Learning Mechanics (HCLM) is a dynamical and information-theoretic framework for open and controlled learning systems. Absorptive capacity theory, from business administration, describes a firm's ability to recognize, assimilate, and apply new information. Institutional dissipation refers to avoidable frictions like administrative overload and coordination failures.

<a id="item-294"></a>
## [Simple DG Methods Strong Baselines for Open Domain Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.18031) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study comprehensively evaluates existing domain generalization (DG) methods in the open domain generalization (ODG) setting, finding that simple methods like CORAL and MMD are competitive with the more complex DAML method. The authors also propose simple extensions of CORAL and MMD that achieve comparable performance to DAML with lower computational cost. This work challenges the notion that complex methods are necessary for ODG, suggesting that simple baselines can be strong and efficient. It provides practitioners with reliable, low-cost starting points for real-world applications involving both domain shift and unknown classes. The study evaluates CORAL, which aligns second-order statistics of source and target distributions, and MMD, which measures distribution discrepancy in a reproducing kernel Hilbert space. The extended versions incorporate ensemble learning and Dirichlet mixup data augmentation from DAML.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Domain generalization (DG) aims to train models that perform well on unseen target domains, while open-set recognition (OSR) handles unknown classes during inference. Open domain generalization (ODG) combines both challenges. DAML is a prior method for ODG but has a complicated learning process.

## [Fatigue Detection via Multi-Source Data and Cross-Domain Imputation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16859) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a novel framework for real-world fatigue detection that integrates heterogeneous multi-source data and uses cross-domain modality imputation to compensate for missing high-quality sensor data in deployment environments. This work addresses a critical gap in fatigue detection by enabling reliable estimation even when high-fidelity sensors are unavailable, which is common in real-world settings like aviation and mining, potentially improving safety in human-machine systems. The framework leverages knowledge from heterogeneous source domains (e.g., controlled lab environments with high-fidelity sensors) to assist detection in the target domain (real-world settings) via cross-domain modality imputation based on shared modalities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

While high-fidelity sensors work well in labs, they often fail in real-world conditions due to noise, lighting, and field-of-view issues. This paper formalizes a deployment-oriented setting where such sensors are unavailable and proposes using cross-domain modality imputation to transfer knowledge from source domains.

## [Heterogeneous Graph Transformer Predicts High-Potential SMEs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.19591) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced SME-HGT, a Heterogeneous Graph Transformer framework that predicts which SBIR Phase I awardees will advance to Phase II funding using only public data, achieving an AUPRC of 0.621, outperforming MLP and R-GCN baselines. This work demonstrates that relational structure among firms, research topics, and funding agencies provides meaningful signal for SME potential assessment, which could help policymakers and early-stage investors identify high-potential companies more efficiently. The heterogeneous graph includes 32,268 company nodes, 124 research topic nodes, and 13 government agency nodes with about 99,000 edges across three semantic relation types. At a screening depth of 100 companies, SME-HGT achieves 89.6% precision with a 2.14 lift over random selection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Small and Medium Enterprises (SMEs) make up 99.9% of U.S. businesses and generate 44% of economic activity, but identifying high-potential ones is challenging. The SBIR program provides Phase I funding for early-stage R&D and Phase II for further development. Heterogeneous Graph Transformers extend graph neural networks to handle multiple node and edge types, capturing complex relationships.

## [Reproducible AutoML Framework for Healthcare Risk Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21528) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose yvsoucom-iterkit, a deterministic AutoML framework that optimizes pipelines with full reproducibility and traceable logs, achieving Weighted-F1 of 0.89 on Pima Indians Diabetes and 0.94 on Stroke datasets. This work addresses reproducibility and interpretability challenges in AutoML, which are critical for clinical adoption, and demonstrates that constraining search spaces to high-impact components can improve performance and stability. The framework evaluated over 18,000 pipeline configurations, revealing a structured but partially redundant search space; ensemble models reduced variance compared to single models, and Friedman testing confirmed significant ranking differences.

<a id="item-299"></a>
## [Attention and Transfer Learning Boost Peach Leaf Damage Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02045) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers proposed deep learning models combining EfficientNet with Convolutional Block Attention Module (CBAM) for peach leaf damage classification, achieving up to 93.3% accuracy on a benchmark dataset and 93% macro F1-score on a local dataset after transfer learning. This work addresses domain shift in agricultural AI, enabling robust crop damage assessment across different fields and conditions, which is critical for early pest and disease management in peach orchards under climate change. The benchmark dataset contains 1,366 peach leaves across six damage categories. EfficientNetB5 with CBAM achieved the best overall accuracy of 93.3%, while EfficientNetB3 with CBAM performed best on the local dataset after fine-tuning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 18, 04:00

**Background**: Domain shift occurs when a model trained on one dataset (source domain) performs poorly on a different but related dataset (target domain) due to differences in image conditions. EfficientNet is a family of convolutional neural networks that use compound scaling to balance depth, width, and resolution for efficient performance. CBAM is a lightweight attention module that enhances feature representation by focusing on important spatial and channel-wise features.
